Feature request for atproto apps: Add and show metadata about which tool was used to eg. post a Bluesky post. Twitter used to do this and it helped discoverability of third party apps. The IndieWeb documents their approach here: indieweb.org/h-x-app /cc @mu.social @pfrazee.com @snarfed.org

<div class="link-info" markdown="1">
**h-x-app**
<p class="link-desc">h-x-app is a proposed microformats vocabulary for marking up data about software applications.</p>
<small>:material-link: https://indieweb.org/h-x-app</small>
</div>
</div>
</a>



</div>

## **5. Post by `@itstally.bsky.social` on 2026-11-09**

<div class="bsky-card" markdown="1">

<div class="bsky-header" markdown="1">

<img src="https://cdn.bsky.app/img/avatar/plain/did:plc:c22amt5vl4rw6ah6w64rejoc/bafkreidonftkeah7avo42kligud3c4a3miji6c573kfip5tdctr3vkpb44" class="bsky-avatar">

<div class="bsky-author-meta" markdown="1">
**Tally** [@itstally.bsky.social](https://bsky.app/profile/did:plc:c22amt5vl4rw6ah6w64rejoc){:target="_blank"}
<br><small>2026-11-09</small>
</div>
</div>

<div class="bsky-content" markdown="1">

Unicode fact from building a character counter: 👍🏽 is one character to you, 2 code points, 4 UTF-16 units, and 8 UTF-8 bytes. Bluesky's 300 limit counts the first. Link and hashtag positions count the last. Most of the bugs I've found live in the gap between them. atdev







</div>

<br><small>2026-11-08</small>
</div>
</div>

<div class="bsky-content" markdown="1">

Thing I learned building a tiny Bluesky client: link and tag positions ("facets") are counted in UTF-8 bytes, not characters. One 🙂 is 4 bytes, so put it before a link and character offsets land 3 bytes early: the wrong text gets linked. Posted with my own one-file client, as a test. atproto







</div>

<div class="bsky-content" markdown="1">

Just lucky that I've always run dropbear ssh daemon inside musl Linux distros like alpine. But we can expect scary exploits to be found in almost everything. I've almost always regretted using musl for anything, as cool and useful as it can be.

<div class="bsky-content" markdown="1">

Your personal dashboard for all of the election night chatter, courtesy of @graze.social (and of course atproto)







<a href="https://siliconflorist.com/2026/11/03/looking-to-keep-track-of-all-of-the-political-chatter-as-the-polls-close-election-night-2026-is-designed-to-do-just-that/" class="bsky-link-preview-wrapper" target="_blank">
<div class="bsky-link-preview" markdown="1">

<img src="https://cdn.bsky.app/img/feed_thumbnail/plain/did:plc:ksjfbxar6io3y6vcheex2cj4/bafkreibxdqcyh6iowx2plgnxycvgkvyxdwftpmzffeaefgt5dr6xniyp4i" class="link-thumb">

<div class="link-info" markdown="1">
**Looking to keep track of all of the political chatter as the polls close…? Election Night 2026 is designed to do just that – Portland Oregon startups, tech, news, events, jobs, and community**
<p class="link-desc">Just a quick reminder that it is, in fact, Election Day for the 2026 midterms. Which means tonight is Election Night 2026. And that’s exactly why our friends at Graze built the site. To monit…</p>

But off the top of my head I've run into: terrible concurrency w/per process connections, garbage and VACUUM overhead/performance, crappy heuristics query planner, WAL replication but DIY failover, no horizontal write scaling, write contention, jittery query latency, etc.







</div>
